These results demonstrate that PFR provides a practical and robust representation for safe and efficient robotic throwing in unstructured environments. A video showcasing our experiments is available at: https://youtu.be/ZZnJf8ua2dE

</details>


### [177] [From Foundation to Application: Improving VLA Models in Practice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06403)
*Wei Wu,Fangjing Wang,Fan Lu,He Sun,Shi Liu,Yunnan Wang,Yibin Yan,Yong Wang,Shuailei Ma,Xinyang Wang,Yibin Liu,Shuai Yang,Tianxiang Zhou,Kejia Zhang,Lei Zhou,Cheng Su,Nan Xue,Bin Tan,Han Zhang,Youchao Zhang,Fei Liao,Xing Zhu,Yujun Shen,Kecheng Zheng*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: LingBot-VLA 2.0 enhances VLA models by improving task generalization, expanding action capabilities, and implementing predictive dynamics for better temporal reasoning, validated by evaluations on the GM-100 benchmark.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the disparity between laboratory and real-world applications of VLA foundation models and improve their practical implementation.

Method: The paper introduces LingBot-VLA 2.0, which features an enhanced data pipeline, accommodates expanded action spaces for multiple robotic configurations, and employs predictive dynamics modeling for future task prediction based on video and depth representation models.

Result: Evaluations on the GM-100 benchmark reveal that LingBot-VLA 2.0 exhibits superior generalization and manipulation capabilities across various robot embodiments, leveraging extensive pretraining data.

Conclusion: The proposed improvements position LingBot-VLA 2.0 as a powerful tool for complex real-world applications involving mobile manipulation across different robotic platforms.

Abstract: Despite recent progress of VLA foundation models, the disparity between laboratory conditions and real-world applications continues to impede their practical implementation. To bridge this gap, we present LingBot-VLA 2.0, which advances LingBot-VLA through improvements in three functional domains. (1) Generalization across tasks and embodiments. Compared to the previous version, we revamp the data processing pipeline and curate around 60,000 hours of data for pretraining, including 50,000 hours of robot trajectories spanning 20 robot configurations and 10,000 hours of egocentric human videos. (2) Expanded action space in addition to dual-arm hardware platforms. In particular, our system accommodates degrees of freedom for the heads, waists, mobile bases, and dexterous hands, thereby empowering the robots to tackle more complex tasks in practical scenarios. (3) Predictive dynamics modeling for improved temporal reasoning. Specifically, we formulate future prediction as a proxy task, facilitated by a video representation model for semantic priors and a depth estimation model for geometric cues. Evaluations on the GM-100 benchmark, conducted in a generalist setting, validate the beneficial impact of these proposed modifications. Furthermore, benefiting from the expanded pretraining data that covers whole-body degrees of freedom, LingBot-VLA-2.0 demonstrates strong cross-embodiment long-horizon mobile manipulation capability across the two robotic platforms.

</details>


### [178] [WristMimic: Full-Body Humanoid Control with Wrist-Guided Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06438)
*Wongyun Yu,Youngwoon Kim,Minsu Cho*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: WristMimic is a framework that separates body motion from hand manipulation in simulations, enabling better object interaction by using wrist dynamics to guide finger actions without direct supervision.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The challenge of accurately simulating human manipulation in physics-based environments, particularly in capturing both body and object motions, necessitates a more effective control framework.

Method: WristMimic employs a wrist-guided control mechanism, using kinematic targets to manage body motion while allowing fingers to learn manipulation through object interactions and contact outcomes, without direct hand pose supervision.

Result: The approach achieves comparable or better performance than methods requiring full finger pose supervision and facilitates flexible retargeting of hand behaviors across different embodiments.

Conclusion: WristMimic effectively bridges kinematic motion control and dynamic hand behavior, promoting versatile interaction capabilities in physics simulations.

Abstract: Retargeting human object interaction demonstrations to physics based simulation requires reproducing not only body motion but also the object motion and contacts that make manipulation succeed. However, position only hand trajectories do not specify the contact forces needed to manipulate objects, and directly tracking them can overconstrain contact rich finger behavior. We introduce WristMimic, a wrist guided whole body control framework that explicitly separates contact free body motion from contact rich hand manipulation. The contact free body and wrist are guided by kinematic pose targets, whereas the fingers are not directly supervised by human hand pose. Instead, they learn grasping and manipulation behaviors from object tracking and contact outcomes. Our key insight is that the wrist is the natural gate between these two regimes. It is largely free from contact and can be tracked kinematically, yet it determines the global hand configuration and places the fingers within reachable grasp affordances. To ensure reliable wrist placement during interaction, we introduce wrist specific reset constraints and reward prioritization. Experiments show that WristMimic matches or surpasses methods using full finger pose supervision while enabling finger agnostic retargeting across diverse hand embodiments.

</details>


### [179] [SIEVE: Structure-Aware Data Selection for Imitation Learning with VLA Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06442)
*Changti Wu,Bin Yu,Zhaolong Shen,Shijie Lian,Xiaopeng Lin,Cong Huang,Zhirui Zhang,Lei Zhang,Kai Chen*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: SIEVE is a structure-aware data selection method for Vision-Language-Action models that improves imitation learning efficiency by maximizing the reuse of visuo-motor primitives and transition interfaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address issues of redundancy, noise, and uneven coverage in large-scale robot demonstration datasets that hinder the performance of VLA models during imitation learning.

Method: SIEVE discovers visuo-motor primitives from segmented trajectories and allocates selection budgets to patterns of compositions to maximize structural exposure. It selects central medoid trajectories for effective imitation.

Result: SIEVE consistently outperforms existing data selection techniques and achieves better performance than full-data training while using only 50% of the demonstrations and training steps.

Conclusion: The findings illustrate that leveraging reusable structures through primitives and transitions is crucial for enhancing the efficiency of VLA imitation learning.

Abstract: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models are typically trained by imitation learning on large-scale robot demonstration datasets, but more data does not necessarily yield better policies due to redundancy, noise, and uneven coverage. Existing data selection methods often assess demonstrations at either the trajectory or state-action level, missing the reusable structures that compose long-horizon behaviors. In this paper, we propose SIEVE, a structure-aware data selection method for VLA imitation learning. SIEVE views demonstrations as compositions of reusable primitives and transition interfaces. It first discovers visuo-motor primitives from segmented trajectories, then allocates selection budgets to composition patterns by maximizing reuse-aware structural exposure under diminishing returns. Finally, it selects medoid trajectories within each composition-pattern bucket to retain central, stable, and imitation-friendly demonstrations. Experiments across multiple datasets, benchmarks, and VLA models show that SIEVE consistently outperforms competitive data selection baselines. Notably, SIEVE can surpass full-data training while using only 50% of demonstrations and 50% of training steps, suggesting that reusable structure, captured through primitives and transitions, is an important signal for efficient VLA imitation learning.

</details>


### [180] [Hilti-Trimble-Oxford Dataset: 360 Visual-Inertial Benchmark with Floor Plan Priors for SLAM and Localization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06464)
*Samuele Centanni,Yuhao Zhang,Yifu Tao,Julien Kindle,Frank Neuhaus,Tilman Koß,Aryaman Patel,Michael Helmberger,Emilia Szymańska,Torben Gräber,Maurice Fallon*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper presents a dataset and benchmark for automated progress monitoring on construction sites, comparing various SLAM and localization systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the challenges in accurate progress monitoring and change detection in construction using cost-effective technologies like 360 cameras and IMUs, as alternatives to expensive LiDAR systems.

Method: A high-quality dataset was created from visual-inertial sequences captured over eight months in an active construction site, with ground truth established via LiDAR-inertial SLAM.

Result: The dataset includes thirty sequences over seven floors, and a challenge demonstrated greater participation in SLAM systems versus floor-plan-referenced localization, revealing the disparity in maturity and performance.

Conclusion: The findings underscore the need for further research in construction localization, stressing the dataset's potential to advance the field.

Abstract: Automated progress monitoring on construction sites is an active area of research and development. Robot and human-carried mapping systems have been developed to build 3D maps of building and infrastructure projects. While LiDAR-based mapping systems achieve high accuracy, the cost of LiDAR can be prohibitive. Consumer-grade cameras with wide field of view ("360 cameras") combined with embedded inertial measurement units (IMUs) provide a cost-effective alternative. To support change detection and progress monitoring, highly accurate visual Simultaneous Localization and Mapping (SLAM) and floor plan-referenced localization systems are required. In this paper we present a high-quality dataset collected at an active construction site, which captures realistic challenges such as variable lighting conditions, moving workers, fast motions, and repetitive structures. The dataset offers thirty visual-inertial sequences recorded across seven floors over an eight-month period of the construction project. Ground truth trajectories were collected using a high quality LiDAR-inertial SLAM system rigidly attached to the 360 camera. Additionally, we report the results of an open research challenge evaluating the best visual SLAM and localization systems from around the world. The Challenge attracted substantially higher participation in SLAM, with 62 teams compared to 22 in floor-plan-referenced localization, reflecting the broader maturity of SLAM methods. The higher errors in localization further highlight the difficulty of this task in construction and point to the need for continued research, which this dataset is intended to support. The dataset and the benchmark are publicly available at: https://hilti-trimble-challenge.com/dataset-2026.

</details>


### [181] [Clustering-Embedded Model Predictive Path Integral Control: Avoiding Averaging-Induced Failure and Enabling Efficient Cluster Selection for Dynamic Obstacles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06499)
*Zidong Liu,Kaixin Chang,Xu Chen*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: The paper introduces Clustering-Embedded MPPI (CE-MPPI), a novel framework to enhance Model Predictive Path Integral (MPPI) control in complex environments by addressing averaging-induced failures through clustering and trajectory optimization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper aims to improve sampling-based motion planning methods, specifically MPPI, by overcoming its limitations in cluttered environments where averaging can lead to collisions or indecision.

Method: CE-MPPI integrates a pruning and clustering stage using density-based spatial clustering (DBSCAN) and a geometric direction feature from collision-derived points to differentiate feasible trajectories from infeasible ones, optimizing for static and dynamic scenarios.

Result: Experimental results indicate that CE-MPPI effectively reduces hesitation in the presence of obstacles and maintains distance from dynamic obstacles, achieving significant improvements in motion planning performance.

Conclusion: The framework demonstrates effective enhancements in obstacle avoidance and path efficiency in both simulated and real-world environments.

Abstract: With the widespread availability of parallel computing hardware, sampling-based motion planning methods such as Model Predictive Path Integral (MPPI) control have become increasingly powerful for complex nonlinear systems in non-smooth task spaces. However, the sampling and forward-simulation pipeline in MPPI suffers from averaging-induced failure in cluttered environments, where the importance-weighted update averages incompatible rollouts and leads to hesitation or even collision when an obstacle lies directly ahead. This paper proposes Clustering-Embedded MPPI (CE-MPPI), a framework that architecturally resolves the averaging-induced failures inherent in standard MPPI within non-convex environments. Rather than simply mitigating interference, CE-MPPI redefines the control law by integrating a high-fidelity pruning and clustering stage. By leveraging density-based spatial clustering of applications with noise (DBSCAN) alongside a novel geometric direction feature that is extracted from collision-derived reference points, the system isolates feasible trajectory modes from the noise of infeasible rollouts. This is paired with an intelligent selection logic that optimizes for minimum cost in static scenes while actively steering opposite to obstacle flux in dynamic environments. Experiments in 2-D JAX-accelerated simulations show that CE-MPPI alleviates obstacle-front hesitation and avoids persistent coupling with moving obstacles in dynamic scenes. In particular, real-world tests on a 6-DoF UR5e manipulator with CUDA-parallel rollouts in Isaac Gym achieve a 48\% reduction in time-to-goal and a 12\% shorter end-effector path.

</details>


### [182] [Hypothesis-driven Model Expansion under Uncertainty for Open-World Robot Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06501)
*Anxing Xiao,Hanbo Zhang,Tianrun Hu,David Hsu*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper proposes an open-world planning framework for service robots to autonomously expand their knowledge in unknown environments through generating, verifying, and updating hypotheses about their world models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is to address the limitations of traditional closed-world planning methods, which struggle with unexpected situations and tasks in autonomous operations of service robots.

Method: The proposed framework maintains uncertainty-aware knowledge expansion and integrates hypothesis verification with goal-reaching planning, utilizing foundation models for generating hypotheses and automated planning for action sequence production.

Result: Experiments in various environments show that the framework allows robots to autonomously expand knowledge and perform effectively in open-world situations.

Conclusion: The findings suggest that incorporating uncertainty-aware model expansion and planning improves the practical deployment of household service robots.

Abstract: We consider an open-world planning setting in which service robots must operate in unknown environments with incomplete knowledge of objects and actions. Traditional closed-world approaches with pre-programmed knowledge bases fail when robots encounter unexpected situations and tasks, posing a fundamental challenge for autonomous knowledge expansion in human environments. In this work, we propose an open-world planning framework that enables robots to automatically generate, verify, and update hypotheses about their abstract world models. Our key insight is to explicitly maintain uncertainty-aware knowledge expansion and integrate hypothesis verification into goal-reaching planning. The framework leverages foundation models to generate initial hypotheses over states and transitions, and applies automated planning to produce action sequences that jointly address hypothesis verification and task execution. Through iterative execution and refinement, the robot expands its knowledge by incorporating verification feedback from the foundation models when hypotheses prove incorrect. Extensive experiments in simulated and real-world environments demonstrate that our framework enables autonomous knowledge expansion and effective operation in open-world settings. These results indicate that integrating uncertainty-aware model expansion from robot foundation models with planning advances the practical deployment of household service robots.

</details>


### [183] [Neural-ESO: A Dual-Pathway Architecture for Provably Robust Learning-Based Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06535)
*Fan Zhang,Richie Suganda,Jinfeng Chen,Wenhua Liu,Hantao Fu,Bin Hu,Qin Lin*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: The paper introduces a Neural Extended State Observer (Neural-ESO) framework that enhances disturbance-rejection in control systems by integrating neural network capabilities with conventional methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve the reliability and robustness of learning-based control systems in the presence of disturbances, particularly in dynamic environments.

Method: A dual-pathway architecture is utilized, comprising a neural network for predictive disturbance estimation and a conventional Extended State Observer for error correction.

Result: The framework demonstrated improved accuracy and robustness in a quadrotor landing task despite strong disturbances and various operational scenarios.

Conclusion: The Neural-ESO framework exhibits superior operational reliability and effective disturbance rejection compared to existing state-of-the-art methods.

Abstract: A learning-enabled disturbance-rejection framework based on a Neural Extended State Observer (Neural-ESO) is presented in this letter. Unlike existing learning-based control methods that largely rely on the learned model once deployed, Neural-ESO adopts a dual-pathway architecture: a predictive pathway uses a neural network to provide a feedforward disturbance estimate that accelerates convergence, while a corrective pathway employs a conventional ESO to compensate prediction errors and prevent over-reliance on the neural component. Using Lyapunov theory and a small-gain analysis, we show that enforcing a Lipschitz bound on the learning component guarantees uniform ultimate boundedness of the closed-loop error dynamics. The proposed framework is validated on a quadrotor landing task subject to strong ground-effect disturbances across normal and out-of-distribution scenarios, demonstrating accuracy-robustness trade-off and greater operational reliability during training, deployment, and transfer compared with state-of-the-art baselines.

</details>


### [184] [UniLM-Nav: A Unified Framework for Zero-Shot Last-Mile Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06537)
*Zhuofan Zhang,Tianxu Wang,Guoxi Zhang,Yixiong Lin,Xilin Wang,Hongming Xu,Qing Li,Song-Chun Zhu,Lifeng Fan*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: UniLM-Nav is introduced as a solution for last-mile navigation in robotics, aiming for zero-shot open-vocabulary capability by decomposing the task into key components integrated with a multimodal large language model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The need for effective last-mile navigation in mobile manipulation due to the limitations of previous methods reliant on manual or task-specific training.

Method: UniLM-Nav employs a unified framework that includes view selection, affordance grounding, and base-pose reasoning, leveraging a multimodal large language model.

Result: UniLM-Nav achieved a performance improvement over MoTo by 3.13 percentage points on the OVMM benchmark, demonstrating the effectiveness of its components and the influence of the MLLM used.

Conclusion: The framework is validated on real-world tasks with a quadruped robot, showcasing its practical applicability and superiority over prior methods.

Abstract: Mobile manipulation requires a robot to navigate to a target object or receptacle and then perform intended manipulation. However, reaching the vicinity of the target does not guarantee a manipulation-ready base pose, a problem known as last-mile navigation. Prior methods for last-mile navigation either rely on manual pose annotation or task-specific training, limiting their scalability to open-vocabulary settings with fine-grained spatial constraints. We propose UniLM-Nav, a unified framework for zero-shot open-vocabulary last-mile navigation. UniLM-Nav decomposes last-mile navigation into view selection, task-conditioned affordance grounding, and geometry-aware base-pose reasoning, all resolved with a shared multimodal large language model (MLLM) backend. Specifically, UniLM-Nav first selects a reference view that best captures the target object or receptacle from recently collected observations. It then grounds task-relevant affordance point in the selected view and lifts the result into the robot-centric coordinate frame. Finally, conditioned on the grounded affordance, task context, and robot geometry, it infers a manipulation-ready base pose for the robot. We evaluate UniLM-Nav on the OVMM benchmark, where it outperforms the previous state-of-the-art method, MoTo, by 3.13 percentage points. Analyses show that the components of our method are crucial to final performance, and that the choice of MLLM also has a substantial effect. We further deploy UniLM-Nav on a Unitree B2 quadruped robot with a 6-DoF Unitree Z1 manipulator, validating its applicability to real-world mobile manipulation tasks.

</details>


### [185] [RynnWorld-Teleop: An Action-Conditioned World Model for Digital Teleoperation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06558)
*Haoyu Zhao,Xingyue Zhao,Hangyu Li,Biao Gong,Kehan Li,Siteng Huang,Xin Li,Deli Zhao,Zhongyu Li*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: RynnWorld-Teleop introduces digital teleoperation, enabling efficient data collection for robot learning by using a generative world model to synthesize actions, resulting in high success rates and effective zero-shot transfer from simulation to real-world tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The bottleneck in scaling robot learning is the reliance on physical teleoperation, limiting data collection to specific hardware and workspaces.

Method: Digital teleoperation utilizes a generative world model driven by an operator's hand-pose to produce high-fidelity videos, allowing for action labeling transferable across different robots and independent of physical constraints. The RynnWorld-Teleop system integrates depth-aware skeletal conditioning and uses a video Diffusion Transformer for real-time generation.

Result: The approach achieves real-time generation at over 40 FPS, enabling effective zero-shot Sim2Real transfer for bimanual tasks, and improves success rates when augmenting real-world datasets with synthesized data.

Conclusion: RynnWorld-Teleop serves as a scalable, high-fidelity data engine for robotic agents, enhancing the capabilities of learning through simulated experiences.

Abstract: Scaling robot learning requires massive, diverse trajectory data, yet collection is currently bottlenecked by physical teleoperation, where every demonstration binds operator time to specific hardware and workspaces. We introduce digital teleoperation, a paradigm that decouples data collection from physical constraints by replacing the real robot with a generative world model. In this framework, an operator's hand-pose stream drives a robot-centric generative world model to synthesize high-fidelity egocentric videos from a single reference image. The recorded pose stream serves as an embodiment-agnostic action label transferable to any target robot via standard retargeting, yielding complete state-action trajectories for imitation learning independent of physical hardware. We instantiate this paradigm in RynnWorld-Teleop, a system that integrates depth-aware skeletal conditioning, progressive human-to-robot training on a video Diffusion Transformer, and streaming autoregressive distillation. This pipeline compresses the generative process into a single-pass inference, enabling 40+ FPS, real-time interactive generation on a single H100 GPU. Policies trained exclusively on RynnWorld-Teleop-generated data achieve effective zero-shot Sim2Real transfer across dexterous and diverse bimanual tasks. Moreover, augmenting real-world datasets with our digitally teleoperated data consistently improves success rates, demonstrating that RynnWorld-Teleop serves as a high-fidelity, scalable data engine for the next generation of robotic agents.

</details>


### [186] [RynnWorld-4D: 4D Embodied World Models for Robotic Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06559)
*Haoyu Zhao,Xingyue Zhao,Siteng Huang,Xin Li,Deli Zhao,Zhongyu Li*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: RynnWorld-4D introduces a generative model for robotic manipulation, integrating RGB, depth, and optical flow for improved 3D structure anticipation and action prediction from single images and language instructions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance robotic manipulation by recognizing scene dynamics and integrating visual with geometric information for better predictive modeling and action execution.

Method: Introduction of RynnWorld-4D, a generative model utilizing RGB-D images and language instructions to produce synchronized RGB frames, depth maps, and optical flow, trained on the large Rynn4DDataset 1.0.

Result: RynnWorld-4D demonstrates coherent 4D predictions, while RynnWorld-4D-Policy shows state-of-the-art performance in real-world bimanual manipulation tasks with high spatial and temporal accuracy.

Conclusion: The developed model effectively narrows the gap between world prediction and robotic policy learning, facilitating advanced manipulation capabilities in robots.

Abstract: Robotic manipulation in the open world requires not only recognizing what a scene looks like, but also anticipating how its 3D structure moves under interaction. We argue that synchronized RGB, depth, and optical flow, namely RGB-DF, provide a physically grounded representation that captures the underlying 4D dynamics of a scene. Compared to 2D pixel videos, this multi-modal synergy aligns visual appearance with geometric structure and temporal motion, creating a representation space significantly closer to the low-level end-effector actions demanded by robotic systems, thereby narrowing the gap between world prediction and policy learning. Building on this insight, we introduce RynnWorld-4D, a generative model that co-produces future RGB frames, depth maps, and optical flow from a single RGB-D image and a language instruction within one unified diffusion process. This 4D world model features a tri-branch architecture that integrates cross-modal attention with frame-wise 3D RoPE, ensuring that appearance, geometry, and motion evolve consistently. To supply training data at scale, we curate Rynn4DDataset 1.0, a massive dataset of over 254.4 million frames across egocentric human and robotic manipulation videos with high-quality pseudo-labels for depth and optical flow. We further propose RynnWorld-4D-Policy, an inverse dynamics head that consumes the internal 4D representations of RynnWorld-4D in a single forward pass, bypassing expensive multi-step denoising, to output robot actions in a closed-loop manner. Experiments show that RynnWorld-4D produces temporally and spatially coherent 4D predictions, and that RynnWorld-4D-Policy achieves state-of-the-art performance on real-world dexterous bimanual manipulation tasks, particularly excelling in tasks demanding spatial precision and temporal coordination.

</details>


### [187] [Embodied Human-Robot Interaction via Acoustics: A MARL Approach with AcoustoBots for Spatial Data Physicalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06563)
*Shiqi Liu,Narsimlu Kemsaram,Prateek Mittal,Pengyuan Wei,Sriram Subramanian*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: AcoustoBots enhances data physicalization through mobile robots using ultrasonic arrays to visually represent urban data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve traditional static data physicalization by integrating mobility and real environmental interaction.

Method: Utilizes TurtleBot3 robots with ultrasonic phased arrays for levitation of particles representing urban metrics; employs a MARL algorithm for navigation and stability control.

Result: Achieved stable particle levitation and height rendering with 90% success in single-robot and 80% in dual-robot operations, while minimizing collisions across trials.

Conclusion: Demonstrates the effectiveness of acoustophoretic levitation as an intuitive communication method for human-robot interaction in urban data visualization.

Abstract: Traditional data physicalization is often static and disconnected from real environments, limiting its ability to convey embodied spatial dynamics and engage users. To address this limitation, we present AcoustoBots, a mobile acoustophoretic data-physicalization platform in which TurtleBot3 robots carry upward-facing 8 x 8 ultrasonic phased arrays. Each array levitates a particle whose height (1-10 cm) encodes a local urban scalar value, such as population density, noise, or traffic. A MARL (Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning) policy based on the Multi-Agent Deep Deterministic Policy Gradient (MADDPG) algorithm, with centralized training and decentralized execution, selects collision-aware navigation actions, while a high-rate Gerchberg-Saxton-Phased Array of Transducers (GS-PAT) acoustic controller maintains trap stability and updates array phases to achieve the commanded height during motion. This creates a closed perception-display-action loop. We evaluate single-robot city-to-city traversal and dual-robot cooperative coverage on a 4 m x 3 m scaled UK map using PhaseSpace-based localization for repeatable multi-robot trials. Results show stable in-motion levitation and consistent, location-dependent height rendering, with task success rates of 90% and 80% for the single and dual-robot regimes, respectively, over 10 trials per regime, and low collision counts. These findings support acoustophoretic levitation as a simple, glanceable, robot-mediated communication cue for embodied human-robot interaction in spatial analytics.

</details>


### [188] [Lift3D-VLA: Lifting VLA Models to 3D Geometry and Dynamics-Aware Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06564)
*Jiaming Liu,Qingpo Wuwu,Nuowei Han,Hao Chen,Zhuoyang Liu,Fan Fei,Yueru Jia,Chenyang Gu,Yandong Guo,Boxin Shi,Shanghang Zhang*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: Lift3D-VLA is a new Vision-Language-Action framework addressing geometric understanding and spatial reasoning for robotic manipulation through explicit 3D point cloud reasoning and temporal action generation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations in existing VLA models regarding 3D information and dynamic action representation necessary for robotic manipulation in real environments.

Method: Lift3D-VLA employs an enhanced 2D model-lifting strategy for geometrically aligning 3D points, introduces Geometry-Centric Masked Autoencoding for dual-objective self-supervision, and implements layer-wise temporal action modeling for consistent action predictions.

Result: Lift3D-VLA achieved significantly higher success rates in both simulated and real-world tasks compared to previous VLA methods, indicating superior performance and generalization.

Conclusion: The proposed framework effectively integrates 3D geometry understanding and temporally coherent action generation, elevating robotic manipulation capabilities.

Abstract: Recently, Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models have demonstrated strong generalization across diverse tasks. However, effective robotic manipulation in physical environments fundamentally requires geometric understanding and spatial reasoning. While some VLA approaches attempt to incorporate 3D information, they are constrained by limited data availability and geometric information loss in current 3D encoding pipelines, and fail to jointly capture 3D geometry and temporally structured actions in dynamic environments. To address these limitations, we introduce Lift3D-VLA, a unified VLA framework that equips models with explicit 3D point cloud reasoning and enables temporally coherent action generation. First, building upon our previous work Lift3D, an enhanced 2D model-lifting strategy is proposed to geometrically align 3D points with pretrained 2D positional embeddings. This design enables direct point-cloud encoding within the VLA vision encoder while minimizing spatial information loss. Based on explicit 3D inputs, we propose Geometry-Centric Masked Autoencoding (GC-MAE), a dual-objective self-supervised framework that reconstructs the current point cloud while predicting its future geometric evolution. This formulation allows the 2D vision encoder to internalize both 3D structure and physical dynamics. To fully exploit 3D representations, we further design layer-wise temporal action modeling, which leverages multiple layers of the LLM to collaboratively predict action chunks, enabling temporally consistent predictions. Across 22 simulated tasks and 8 real-world manipulation tasks, Lift3D-VLA achieves 10.8% and 11.1% higher mean success rates on MetaWorld and RLBench than the best-performing prior VLA methods, and outperforms the strongest real-world baseline by 4 percentage points, while exhibiting stronger generalization to out-of-distribution perturbations.

</details>


<div id='cs.AR'></div>

# cs.AR [[Back]](#toc)

### [189] [Neuromorphic Silicon Neuron Controller for Adaptive Deep Brain Stimulation in Parkinson's Disease](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05453)
*Md Abu Bakr Siddique,Jakub Orłowski,Yan Zhang,Hongyu An*

Main category: cs.AR

TL;DR: The SiLIF-DBS controller demonstrates an efficient method for adaptive deep brain stimulation in Parkinson's disease with low power consumption and effective beta activity suppression.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a low-power, implantable integrated circuit for adaptive deep brain stimulation that effectively manages motor symptoms in Parkinson's disease.

Method: The SiLIF-DBS controller is built using CMOS technology, incorporating a computational model for closed-loop validation with STN-LFPs and Beta ARV as the control biomarker.

Result: The controller suppresses pathological beta activity while using only 25% of the power required for open-loop stimulation, achieving a suppression efficiency of 5.85%/μW.

Conclusion: The SiLIF-DBS controller offers a significant advancement in power-efficient aDBS solutions for Parkinson's disease management.

Abstract: Parkinson's disease (PD) affects millions worldwide and causes severe motor symptoms. Adaptive deep brain stimulation (aDBS) delivers physiologically informed stimulation that can track fluctuations in PD motor symptoms, enabling more intelligent DBS control. However, most existing aDBS approaches are primarily algorithm- and software-driven, with limited efforts toward circuit realization, particularly low-power and implantable integrated circuits. This paper presents the Silicon Leaky Integrate-and-Fire Deep Brain Stimulation (SiLIF-DBS) controller, a neuromorphic silicon neuron stimulator implemented with metal-oxide-semiconductor (CMOS) technology. For system-level evaluation, a simplified computational model of the SiLIF-DBS controller is derived and embedded within a Parkinsonian cortico-basal ganglia framework for closed-loop validation. The system is driven by beta-band subthalamic nucleus local field potentials (STN-LFPs), with their average rectified value (Beta ARV) used as the control biomarker. Our SiLIF-DBS controller for aDBS suppresses pathological beta activity while consuming only 25% of the power required by open-loop stimulation and achieving a suppression efficiency of $5.85\%$/$μ$W. Overall, our SiLIF-DBS controller achieves strong beta suppression at substantially reduced power, delivering high suppression efficiency that demonstrates it is a viable foundation for low-power implantable aDBS.

</details>


### [190] [BitFair: A 12nm Bit-Serial CNN Accelerator with Learnable Early Termination and Adaptive Bit Ordering for Ultra-Low-Power XR Vision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05445)
*Ang Li,Chang Gao*

Main category: cs.AR

TL;DR: BitFair is a co-designed software-hardware accelerator that improves energy-efficient neural network inference in XR wearables by implementing bit-level early termination and adaptive bit ordering.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The need for XR wearables to operate within strict power and latency constraints necessitates efficient neural network inference methods.

Method: BitFair employs learnable thresholds for early termination of neural network computations and adaptive ordering of bits to prioritize informative ones during processing.

Result: The accelerator achieves sub-millisecond latency and high energy efficiency, with significant improvements in energy and accuracy over existing solutions.

Conclusion: BitFair demonstrates enhanced performance for XR applications by addressing the limitations of traditional architectures, achieving notable efficiency gains and superior accuracy.

Abstract: Extended Reality (XR) wearables require always-on perception within tight power envelopes of a few watts and motion-to-photon latency budgets below 20 ms, leaving only a few milliseconds for neural-network inference. Bit-serial computing is attractive for such energy-efficient neural network acceleration, but many existing architectures still process all bits even when ReLU sets the final output to zero. This paper presents BitFair, a software-hardware co-designed bit-serial CNN accelerator with learnable bit-level early termination and adaptive bit ordering, working under the ultra-low-power and strict latency requirements of XR applications. BitFair exploits dynamic bit-level sparsity by learning per-layer thresholds that trigger early termination when partial sums reliably predict that the final ReLU output will be zero. Furthermore, it searches for layer-wise bit orders that prioritize informative bits, maximizing early termination without sacrificing accuracy. A GlobalFoundries 12nm FinFET implementation with a core area of 0.34 mm^2, 104 KB on-chip memory, and voltage scaling from 0.55 to 0.70 V achieves sub-millisecond latency, up to 117.0 BTOPS/W, and 0.07 pJ/SOP. On IBM DVS128 Gesture and N-MNIST, BitFair achieves 96.5% and 97.7% accuracy, respectively, while improving effective energy efficiency by 4.0-22.1x and accuracy by up to 9.2% over prior fabricated XR vision accelerators.

</details>


### [191] [Is Your NPU Ready for LLMs? Dissecting the Hidden Efficiency Bottlenecks in Mobile LLM Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05475)
*Guanyu Cai,Ruiming Tian,Lang Yang,Zhouhong Ren,Jinliang Yuan,Lingkun Li,Jiliang Wang*

Main category: cs.AR

TL;DR: This study investigates the energy efficiency and performance of LLMs on mobile devices through a comprehensive measurement analysis across various frameworks and hardware backends, providing insights and a configuration to optimize energy use.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the bottlenecks in deploying Large Language Models on mobile devices caused by hardware inefficiency and enhance privacy and latency.

Method: A cross-layer measurement study is conducted using a tool called PowerBench for fine-grained profiling of energy attribution across five frameworks and three hardware types (CPU, GPU, NPU).

Result: The study reveals performance gaps due to frameworks, distinct advantages of different hardware types in various tasks, and identifies significant energy waste due to suboptimal resource management.

Conclusion: An energy-oriented configuration for mobile LLM inference is proposed, which could potentially lower energy consumption by 54.8% for NPU backends.

Abstract: Deploying Large Language Models (LLMs) on mobile devices enhances privacy and reduces latency, but is severely bottlenecked by hardware inefficiency. We present the first comprehensive, cross-layer measurement study of mobile LLM inference, uniquely spanning five mainstream frameworks (e.g., llama.cpp, GENIE) and three hardware backends (CPU, GPU, NPU). To enable this analysis, we develop PowerBench, a fine-grained profiling tool that provides the first backend-specific energy attribution, moving beyond traditional device-level measurements. Our study yields three critical insights: (1) Framework-induced performance gaps are substantially amplified on NPUs, reaching up to 10x using custom operators due to divergent offloading and quantization strategies. (2) We identify a distinct phase split where NPUs excel at compute-bound prefilling, while CPUs outperform all other backends in memory-bound decoding. This is driven by the NPU's preference for large, fixed-shape workloads, which conflicts with the small-kernel, dynamic nature of decoding. (3) Backend-specific profiling uncovers substantial scheduling headroom missed by prior work. Suboptimal thread configurations, uncoordinated NPU sleep latencies, and CPU polling intervals result in up to 40% energy waste. Leveraging these findings, we present an energy-oriented best-practice configuration for mobile LLM inference. We estimate that this configuration could reduce energy consumption by up to 54.8% on the NPU backend across three datasets.

</details>


### [192] [NEMESIS: NEtlist-Driven Modeling and Equation Synthesis with Inversion-Aware SPICE Anchoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05657)
*Subhadip Ghosh,Ramesh Harjani,Sachin S. Sapatnekar*

Main category: cs.AR

TL;DR: NEMESIS is a multimodal framework for OTA design that optimizes the balance between speed and accuracy by using LLMs and SPICE.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance the design process of operational transconductance amplifiers (OTAs) by improving speed and accuracy in performance evaluations.

Method: NEMESIS identifies circuit primitives from an OTA netlist and generates performance equations, refining them through a SPICE-based loop, leveraging previous runs when possible.

Result: Demonstrated with five OTA topologies in a 65nm PDK, yielding SPICE-verified equations with less than 7% average relative error and a speedup of ~4622x compared to full SPICE evaluations.

Conclusion: NEMESIS significantly accelerates the OTA design process while maintaining good accuracy, facilitating efficient circuit design.

Abstract: This work presents NEMESIS, a multimodal framework for operational transconductance amplifier (OTA) design using large language models (LLMs). NEMESIS strikes a balance between fast, approximate analytical models vs. accurate, computationally expensive SPICE evaluations. Given an OTA netlist and schematic, NEMESIS first identifies circuit primitives and then generates progressively more accurate performance equations. The framework begins with equations retrieved from the prior invocations of NEMESIS to structurally similar OTAs, if available; otherwise, it uses the LLM to derive the initial equations directly from the circuit input. These equations are iteratively refined via a SPICE-based repair loop. In a commercial 65nm PDK, NEMESIS is demonstrated on five OTA topologies, producing SPICE-verified equations across biasing ranges with <7% average relative error and a post-convergence evaluation speedup of ~4622x over full SPICE-based evaluation.

</details>


### [193] [Boosting FPGA Performance with Direct BRAM-DSP Paths](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05756)
*Jiajun Hu,Ruthwik Reddy Sunketa,Andrew Boutros,Aman Arora*

Main category: cs.AR

TL;DR: This paper presents a lightweight architectural enhancement for FPGA designs that improves data movement efficiency between memory (BRAM) and compute units (DSPs), specifically targeting deep learning workloads, which reduces wirelength and enhances performance without significant overhead or changes to existing tools.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the performance bottleneck caused by inefficient data transfers between BRAMs and DSPs in FPGA designs for deep learning applications.

Method: The paper introduces a dedicated direct connection between BRAM and DSP blocks and enhances the placement algorithm to recognize these macro blocks.

Result: The proposed enhancements lead to improvements of up to +25% in maximum frequency (Fmax) and a reduction of -49% in wirelength on common deep learning layer designs.

Conclusion: This architectural change has minimal area and delay overhead, maintains compatibility with the baseline architecture, and results in negligible impact on the quality-of-results (QoR) in non-deep learning benchmarks.

Abstract: Efficient data movement between memory and compute units is a key performance bottleneck in modern FPGA designs, particularly for deep learning (DL) workloads. In typical FPGA architectures, data transfers between block RAMs (BRAMs) and digital signal processing units (DSPs) must traverse the global routing network, leading to increased wirelength, routing congestion, and critical-path delays. Prior work has explored in- and near-BRAM compute architectures to mitigate these issues, but such solutions often require fundamental changes to FPGA architecture and CAD tools, limiting their commercial viability. This paper proposes a lightweight architectural enhancement that introduces a dedicated direct connection between BRAM and DSP blocks, enabling BRAM data to be consumed by DSPs without passing through the global interconnect. We also enhance the placement algorithm to recognize these BRAM-DSP macro blocks. The proposed architectural change incurs negligible area and delay overhead and does not affect non-DL benchmarks, while the proposed CAD remains compatible with the baseline architecture, where it yields negligible change in quality-of-results (QoR). On an Agilex-10-like FPGA, the proposed architecture and CAD updates deliver up to +25% Fmax and -49% wirelength on common DL layer designs.

</details>


### [194] [GPU-Accelerated Effective Resistance Analysis for 3D IC Power Delivery Network](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05818)
*Jingchao Hu,Cheng Zhuo,Zhou Jin*

Main category: cs.AR

TL;DR: The paper presents a GPU-accelerated framework for analyzing effective resistance in 3D integrated circuit power delivery networks, achieving significant speed improvements while ensuring accuracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The increasing complexity of 3D IC designs and power delivery demands necessitate efficient analysis of effective resistance to maintain reliable power delivery networks.

Method: A GPU-accelerated framework is developed for effective resistance analysis, allowing for faster computation of power delivery metrics.

Result: The framework demonstrates a speedup of 5 to 6 orders of magnitude compared to traditional methods, with minimal error in resistance calculations.

Conclusion: This approach significantly improves the efficiency of analyzing power delivery networks in 3D ICs, addressing challenges posed by poorly planned TSVs.

Abstract: Three-dimensional (3D) integration is a critical technique for enhancing transistor density, improving power efficiency, and reducing interconnect delays. However, as current demands and design complexity increase, power deliver networks (PDNs) are facing growing challenges.Careful planning of through-silicon vias (TSVs) is essential for ensuring reliable PDNs, where effective resistance serves as a vital metric for the reliability. Ill-planned TSVs often cause 3D IC with unevenly distributed effective resistance and consequently severer IR Drop.In this paper, we propose a GPU-accelerated framework on accurate effective resistance analysis for early stage 3D IC PDNs. The proposed framework achieves a speedup of 5 to 6 orders of magnitude compared to the conventional direct solver, while maintaining negligible deviations in both maximum and average relative errors.

</details>


<div id='eess.SP'></div>

### [195] [Replicating the Signature: Unsupervised Targeted Impersonation Attack on RF Fingerprinting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05549)
*Haytham Albousayri,Bechir Hamdaoui*

Main category: eess.SP

TL;DR: The paper introduces a framework for impersonation attacks on RF Fingerprinting systems, demonstrating its effectiveness over existing methods under realistic conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the limitations of existing impersonation attacks on RF Fingerprinting systems, particularly those that require full access to victim classifiers, making the attack scenario more realistic and practical.

Method: Utilizes unsupervised learning for estimating hardware-specific impairments and signal processing techniques to synthesize adversarial signals from different transceiver hardware.

Result: Demonstrates an over 80% increase in targeted attack success rates compared to existing methodologies, even in challenging deployment conditions such as LoS and NLoS scenarios.

Conclusion: The proposed framework significantly enhances impersonation attack effectiveness while ensuring robustness against various constraints, indicating a need for improved defenses in RF Fingerprinting systems.

Abstract: This paper presents a novel impersonation attack framework that aims to fool RF Fingerprinting (RFFP) identification systems by synthesizing signals that replicate the hardware-specific impairments of a target device. Our framework leverages unsupervised learning to enable accurate impairment estimation, combined with signal processing-based generation to synthesize high-fidelity adversarial signals. Unlike prior works that assume full access to the legitimate (victim) RFFP classifier, we consider a more realistic attack strategy where the adversary performs the attack from a completely different transceiver hardware. We further evaluate our proposed attack under realistic and challenging deployment settings, including over-the-air transmission in both Line-of-Sight (LoS) and Non-Line-of-Sight (NLoS) scenarios. Extensive experiments conducted on a Bluetooth Low Energy (BLE) device testbed demonstrate that our attacks remain highly effective even under severe access constraints, significantly outperforming existing baselines in terms of targeted attack success rates by over 80%. We additionally analyze the effects of cross-domain generalization, signal representation mismatch, and classifier diversity, highlighting the robustness and

</details>


### [196] [DBNN: Neural Spike Classification Using a Deep Binarized Neural Network](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05590)
*Binyi Ren,Luca M. Meyer,Majid Zamani*

Main category: eess.SP

TL;DR: The paper presents a hardware-oriented deep binarized neural network (DBNN) for spike sorting in implantable brain-computer interfaces, achieving high accuracy (98.7%) with low power and hardware cost.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop an efficient spike sorting system that minimizes power consumption and hardware complexity while maintaining accurate neural decoding for implantable devices.

Method: A DBNN with two binarized layers (256 neurons each) processes 16-sample spike waveforms, enabling low-cost implementation and multiplier-free inference.

Result: The DBNN achieved a median classification accuracy of 98.7% on both synthetic and in-vivo datasets, with an FPGA prototype demonstrating low compute latency (0.01 ms) and minimal resource usage (0.014 mm2 area, 122 nW power at 20 kHz).

Conclusion: The proposed DBNN offers a favorable trade-off between accuracy and implementation cost, making it suitable for low-power, real-time neural spike sorting applications in implantable devices.

Abstract: Implantable brain-computer interfaces require on-node spike sorting to reduce telemetry bandwidth and power while maintaining reliable neural decoding. This paper presents a hardware-oriented deep binarized neural network (DBNN) spike-sorting system with two binarized hidden layers with 256 neurons and a fixed-point output layer to enable multiplier-free inference dominated by sign-controlled accumulation and bit-wise logic. The proposed classifier operates on compact 16-sample spike waveforms to reduce the implementation cost (16-256-256-3) and achieves a median classification accuracy of 98.7% on both synthetic and in-vivo datasets. An FPGA prototype on a Cyclone V device operates at 50 MHz and requires 528 cycles per spike, corresponding to a 0.01 ms compute latency, while consuming 828 ALMs and 1023 registers with zero DSP blocks. For ASIC feasibility, the DBNN is implemented using FreePDK45-based flow; synthesis in Synopsys Design Compiler indicates an estimated silicon area of 0.014 mm2 and an operating power of 122 nW at 20 kHz under a 1.1 V supply. These results demonstrate that the proposed DBNN spike sorter offers a favorable trade-off between accuracy and implementation cost, supporting low-power, implantable neural interfaces. Overall, the proposed DBNN spike sorter achieves high accuracy (98.7%) with extremely low hardware cost (0.014 mm2, 122 nW at 20 kHz) and multiplier-free operation, making it suitable for low-power, implantable neural interfaces. This paper introduces the first DBNN designed for real-time neural spike sorting, striking an excellent balance between input data size and network complexity.

</details>


### [197] [A Hardware-Aware Open-Source Framework for Design Space Exploration of Mixed-Signal Spiking Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06456)
*Sayma Nowshin Chowdhury,Vineeta Nair,Taseen Forhad,Aishwarya Natarajan,Corey Hart,Sahil Shah*

Main category: eess.SP

TL;DR: An open-source hardware-aware simulation framework for mixed-signal spiking neural networks (SNNs) facilitates design exploration by analyzing various neuron and synapse configurations and their performance metrics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop energy-efficient neuromorphic computing tools that accurately reflect the mixed-signal hardware behavior for effective system-level design exploration.

Method: The framework allows for comparative analysis across different neuron models (LIF, HH, AH), utilizing non-volatile analog synapses, while integrating device-level nonlinearities into PyTorch for real parameter optimization.

Result: Evaluation on standard benchmarks reveals classification accuracy alongside hardware metrics, enabling a comprehensive assessment for neuron-synapse configurations.

Conclusion: This framework supports informed design decisions by correlating accuracy and energy efficiency with hardware constraints, enhancing neuromorphic system development.

Abstract: Energy-efficient neuromorphic computing at the edge requires simulation tools that can capture the non-ideal behavior of mixed-signal spiking neural network (SNN) hardware while supporting system-level design exploration. This work presents an open-source hardware-aware simulation framework for mixed-signal SNNs that enables comparative analysis across neuron, synapse and architecture choices. The framework supports multiple neuron models, including Leaky Integrate-and-Fire (LIF), Hodgkin-Huxley (HH) and Axon-Hillock (AH), together with non-volatile analog synapses based on floating-gate transistors and ReRAM devices. By incorporating device-level nonlinearities directly into PyTorch-based training and inference, the tool enables optimization of physical synaptic parameters rather than idealized abstract weights. The framework is evaluated on standard neuromorphic benchmarks, including N-MNIST, DVS Gesture and Spiking Heidelberg Digits (SHD). For each model dataset configuration, it reports classification accuracy together with hardware-oriented metrics such as silicon area, power consumption and quantization sensitivity. These capabilities enable cross-layer design space exploration and help identify neuron-synapse configurations that best satisfy application-specific constraints on accuracy, energy efficiency, area and hardware fidelity.

</details>


### [198] [Unexpected Far-Near-Far Transition in Mobile Near Field Terahertz Communications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05630)
*Peng Zhang,Hanmei Yuan,Zhe Wang,Vitaly Petrov,Emil Björnson*

Main category: eess.SP

TL;DR: This paper investigates the complex transitions between near-field and far-field propagation in THz communication from elevated access points, highlighting potential multiple transitions based on user movement and deployment geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the propagation behavior of THz frequencies in real deployment scenarios, particularly regarding the near-field to far-field transitions that can occur multiple times due to user movement and access point elevation.

Method: The authors derive analytical conditions for the transitions between near-field and far-field regimes for tilted uniform linear arrays (ULA) and uniform planar arrays (UPA) serving a user, and use numerical simulations for validation.

Result: Numerical results confirm the derived analytical conditions and illustrate that the transition behavior is highly dependent on the geometry of the deployment, with potential for transitions to occur at lower frequencies as well.

Conclusion: The study emphasizes the need to reconsider traditional models of near-field and far-field transitions in practical THz communication scenarios due to the presence of multiple transitions influenced by access point geometry and user motion.

Abstract: At THz frequencies, the radiative near-field distance can be sufficiently large to matter in real deployments. Existing near-field formulas are often understood in a simple way: as the link distance decreases, the propagation regime is expected to change only once, i.e., from far field to near field. This paper shows that this intuition can fail for an elevated access point with downward tilt serving a ground user moving along the ground. Along such a path, the link distance and the viewing angle change together, so the near-field to far-field transition may take place more than once, creating an unexpected far-near-far transition. In this paper, we derive analytical conditions for when this transition occurs for tilted ULA-to-point and UPA-to-point scenarios and compute the corresponding transition point(s) on the ground. Numerical results validate the analysis and further show that this behavior depends strongly on the deployment geometry and can also arise at lower frequencies.

</details>


### [199] [Sensor-Adaptive Infrared Spectral Reconstruction with Plug-and-Play Diffusion Priors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05636)
*Alireza Siyavashi,Jon Schlipf,Sebastian Reiter,Inga Fischer,Christian Wenger,Christian Herglotz*

Main category: eess.SP

TL;DR: The paper presents a compact hyperspectral sensing solution using a pseudoinverse-guided diffusion model for spectrum reconstruction from narrowband photocurrents, achieving high accuracy and sensor adaptability without retraining.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a more accessible and practical solution for hyperspectral sensing that overcomes the limitations of existing bulky and expensive spectrometers, particularly for mobile applications.

Method: The proposed method, ΠGDM, employs a denoising diffusion probabilistic model (DDPM) for spectral reconstruction and utilizes pseudoinverse projection for consistency with photocurrent measurements, enabling sensor adaptability.

Result: The method achieves an average estimation error of 1.502%, surpassing traditional methods such as Tikhonov, Gaussian, compressive-sensing, and multilayer perceptron (MLP) techniques, while also providing calibrated uncertainty estimates through Monte Carlo sampling.

Conclusion: The proposed ΠGDM offers an accurate, efficient, and compact alternative for spectral recovery suitable for use in resource-limited environments.

Abstract: Hyperspectral sensing enables material identification; however, state-of-the-art spectrometers are costly and bulky, which limits their use in mobile applications. We address this by proposing sparse spectrum reconstruction from narrowband photocurrents using a pseudoinverse-guided diffusion model (ΠGDM). With ΠGDM we use a denoising diffusion probabilistic model (DDPM) to reconstruct the spectrum, which is trained on a large public spectral dataset to learn realistic spectral priors, eliminating the need for paired sensor measurements. At inference, ΠGDM alternates reverse-diffusion denoising steps with pseudoinverse projection to enforce consistency with measured photocurrents via the calibrated responsivity matrices of sensors. Consequently, our method is sensor-adaptive: when detector arrays change, we simply substitute the responsivity matrix in the pseudoinverse projection without retraining of the diffusion model. The resulting computational spectrometer achieves 1.502% average estimation error, outperforming Tikhonov, Gaussian, compressive-sensing, and multilayer perceptron (MLP) baselines, while providing calibrated uncertainty estimates via Monte Carlo sampling from different random initializations of ΠGDM. Summarizing, our approach offers an accurate, compact alternative for spectral recovery on resource-constrained platforms.

</details>


### [200] [Rydberg Atomic Quantum Radio: A Comprehensive Survey From Wireless Communication Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05931)
*Yiyue Xiang,Neng Ye,Qihao Peng,Junrui Zhao,Qu Luo,Kai Yang,Jianping An,Pei Xiao*

Main category: eess.SP

TL;DR: Survey on Rydberg atomic quantum radios (RAQ) addressing challenges in space-air-ground-sea networks, covering mechanisms, architecture, techniques to enhance performance, and future directions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To meet the demands of next-generation SAGSIN, advanced RF receivers are needed, and RAQ radios offer a quantum-enabled alternative to conventional technologies.

Method: Comprehensive review of RAQ radios, covering quantum mechanisms, architectures, performance enhancement techniques, and communication theory connections.

Result: Review emphasizes enhanced sensitivity, bandwidth, and frequency, highlighting trade-offs and analyzing RAQ-enabled technologies and deployment scenarios.

Conclusion: Identifies challenges and suggests future research directions for RAQ radios in evolving communication landscapes.

Abstract: Next-generation space-air-ground-sea integrated networks (SAGSIN) impose unprecedented demands on advanced radio frequency (RF) receivers for full-spectrum agility, ultra-high sensitivity, and anti-jamming resilience, pushing conventional electronic receivers to their physical limits. To address these challenges, the Rydberg atomic quantum (RAQ) radio has emerged as a promising quantum-enabled receiver paradigm that directly maps electromagnetic fields onto atomic quantum states, offering an alternative to alleviate bottlenecks of conventional RF front ends. To provide a clear research roadmap, this survey presents a comprehensive review of RAQ radios by bridging atomic physics and wireless communications. Specifically, we first introduce the underlying quantum mechanisms, representative architectures, and atomic response models of RAQ radio. On this basis, state-of-the-art techniques for enhancing sensitivity, instantaneous bandwidth, and operating frequency are systematically reviewed, with particular emphasis on the inherent trade-offs among these key metrics. To connect quantum response with communication theory, we further analyze equivalent channel modeling frameworks for characterizing systematic performance limits. From the wireless communication perspective, some RAQ-enabled advanced technologies including cognitive, interference-resilient, low-frequency and multiple-input multiple-output (MIMO) communications are reviewed, alongside emerging deployment scenarios such as satellite networks, integrated sensing and communications, and reconfigurable intelligent surface-assisted systems. Finally, we identify open challenges and provide potential future directions of RAQ radio to inspire the further exploration.

</details>


### [201] [Cell-Level Channel Shaping for Rydberg Atomic Quantum Receivers in Satellite Uplinks With Doppler-Enabled Superheterodyne Reception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05979)
*Qihao Peng,Qu Luo,Kezhi Wang,Cunhua Pan,Pei Xiao,Trung Q. Duong,Jiangzhou Wang*

Main category: eess.SP

TL;DR: Proposes a self-superheterodyne Rydberg uniform array receiver to enhance satellite uplink communications by utilizing Doppler shifts to generate intermediate-frequency signals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the challenges in satellite uplink communications and improve capacity and performance using innovative receiver technology.

Method: Development of a near-field local oscillator synthesis model and deriving a closed-form RF-to-optical conversion to bridge satellite signals and cell-level responses.

Result: Demonstrated through simulations that the proposed receiver architecture optimizes channel characteristics, reduces inter-user correlation, and enhances capacity over traditional methods.

Conclusion: The self-superheterodyne Rydberg array receiver offers significant potential for future satellite communication systems by providing improved capacity and adaptability.

Abstract: In this paper, we propose a self-superheterodyne Rydberg uniform array receiver for satellite uplink communications, in which the Doppler shift naturally induced by satellite motion is exploited to generate the intermediate-frequency signal. We first develop a near-field local oscillator (LO) synthesis model and characterize the spatially varying LO electric field across the Rydberg vapor cells. Based on a vapor-cell-center approximation, a closed-form radio frequency (RF)-to-optical conversion is derived, establishing an explicit bridge between the incident satellite signal and the LO-induced cell-level response. The derived model reveals that the programmable LO serves as an analog-domain channel-shaping mechanism by controlling the cell-level transduction gain, phase response, and phase-matching behavior. Building upon this equivalent channel model, we formulate an LO design problem that maximizes the Shannon capacity of the effective channel, and develop an efficient optimization algorithm for the LO amplitudes and phases. Simulation results demonstrate that the vapor-cell transduction can reshape the effective channel, adjust the beam-pattern alignment, and moderately reduce the inter-user correlation under suitable LO configurations. Furthermore, the proposed LO design significantly improves the achievable capacity over benchmark schemes, offering a promising self-superheterodyne Rydberg architecture for future satellite communication systems.

</details>


### [202] [Mixture-of-Experts Deep Reinforcement Learning for Reliability-Constrained Energy-Efficient PDCCH Monitoring in Internet of Thing Device](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06084)
*Yue Xiu,Ning Wei,Zixian Song,Tianyu Liu*

Main category: eess.SP

TL;DR: This paper addresses energy consumption in 5G IoT devices due to continuous monitoring of the PDCCH, proposing a predictive scheme to optimize monitoring based on scheduling grants while ensuring reliability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The need to reduce energy consumption in 5G IoT devices while ensuring reliable communication and minimizing missed scheduling grants.

Method: The authors formulate a reliability-constrained energy minimization problem and propose a MoE-IOHMM-based predictive monitoring scheme to optimize PDCCH monitoring decisions.

Result: Simulation results demonstrate that the proposed method significantly lowers energy usage compared to traditional continuous monitoring while keeping the false negative rate within acceptable limits.

Conclusion: The MoE-IOHMM-based approach successfully balances energy efficiency and reliability in PDCCH monitoring for 5G IoT devices.

Abstract: The continuous monitoring of the physical downlink control channel (PDCCH) is a major source of energy consumption in fifth-generation (5G) Internet of thing device (IoT-D), since the UE has to blindly detect downlink control information even when no valid scheduling grant is present. Although predictive dynamic power management can reduce unnecessary receiver activity by skipping PDCCH monitoring in grant-free slots, aggressive sleeping may lead to missed grants and degrade reception reliability. To address this tradeoff, this paper formulates UE-side PDCCH monitoring as a reliability-constrained long-term energy minimization problem. Specifically, the IoT-D determines, before observing the actual scheduling outcome, whether to monitor the PDCCH or switch the receiver chain into a low-power state. The objective is to minimize the long-term average energy consumption, including receiver operating energy, component switching energy, and prediction-related computational energy, while ensuring that the false negative rate of scheduling-grant detection remains below a prescribed threshold. The resulting problem is non-convex due to the bursty and temporally correlated nature of grant arrivals, and the binary monitoring decisions coupled by a long-term reliability constraint. To solve this problem, we propose a mixture-of-experts input-output hidden Markov model (MoE-IOHMM)-based predictive monitoring scheme, where multiple IO-HMM experts capture heterogeneous grant-arrival patterns and a gating network adaptively combines their predictions. Simulation results show that the proposed scheme effectively reduces IoT-D-side energy consumption compared with always-on PDCCH monitoring and conventional predictive baselines, while maintaining the false negative rate below the prescribed reliability threshold.

</details>


### [203] [Bending Beam for THz Wireless Networks: Fundamental, Design Issue, and Prototype](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06294)
*Aoran Liu,Xin Tong Hu,Weidong Mei,Ya Fei Wu,Ke Liu,Boyu Ning,Zhi Chen,Rui Zhang*

Main category: eess.SP

TL;DR: The paper reviews bending beams for THz wireless communication, covering beamforming methods, potential applications, challenges, and a hardware demonstration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore bending beams' potential to enhance THz wireless communication and sensing by overcoming physical obstructions.

Method: The paper compares existing designs for bending beams and discusses practical implementations, including challenges like channel modeling and codebook design.

Result: The review identifies key applications and challenges in using bending beams for THz communications, and presents a hardware demonstration that shows their efficacy.

Conclusion: Bending beams can significantly improve THz wireless communication systems, offering advantages over traditional beamforming techniques.

Abstract: Bending beams, characterized by their non diffracting and self-healing properties in the near field, offer a new approach to bypass blockage in terahertz (THz) wireless communication and sensing. However, the investigations of bend ing beams in the context of wireless communications still remain at an early stage. This article provides a state-of-the-art review of the fundamentals and key application scenarios of bending beams in THz wireless communications and sensing. We first present and compare the existing beamforming design and practical hardware implementation methods for bending beams. Next, we discuss potential applications of bending beams in wireless communica tions and sensing and identify their associated challenges, such as blocked channel modeling, bending beam training, codebook design, etc. Finally, a hardware demonstration of bending beam over THz frequency bands is presented, validating the advantages of bending beam over conventional beamfocusing.

</details>


### [204] [Joint Probabilistic and Geometric Constellation Shaping for Complexity-Constrained Direct Detection Optical Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06300)
*Rodrigo Fischer,Shrinivas Chimmalgi,Andrej Rode,Laurent Schmalen*

Main category: eess.SP

TL;DR: The paper presents a reinforcement learning approach for joint probabilistic and geometric constellation shaping aimed at enhancing the performance of complexity-constrained receivers in optical communication systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve the performance of direct detection optical signal receivers under complexity constraints by utilizing advanced shaping techniques.

Method: The authors evaluate a reinforcement learning-based method for joint probabilistic and geometric constellation shaping in a simulated optical communication system.

Result: The technique was demonstrated successfully in a simulated scenario of a 56 GBd, 2.2 km C-band direct-detection system, showing significant effectiveness.

Conclusion: The proposed method effectively enhances joint equalization and demodulation processes in complexity-constrained optical receivers.

Abstract: We evaluate joint probabilistic and geometric constellation shaping via reinforcement learning for complexity-constrained joint equalization and demodulation of direct detection optical signals. We demonstrate the proposed technique in a simulated 56 GBd, 2.2 km C-band direct-detection system, demonstrating its effectiveness for complexity-constrained receivers.

</details>


<div id='cs.SE'></div>

# cs.SE [[Back]](#toc)

### [205] [KAT-Coder-V2.5 Technical Report](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05471)
*Bo Huang,Fengxiang Li,Hao Xu,Haoyang Huang,Hongyi Fu,Jinhua Hao,Kun Yuan,Minglei Zhang,Pengcheng Xu,Shiyang Liu,Wenhao Zhuang,Yuze Shi,Zongxian Feng,Chao Wang,Cheng He,Chongling Rao,Deyu Cao,Fan Yang,Gang Xiong,Haochen Liu,Jiabao Li,Jian Liang,Jinghui Jia,Jingwen Chang,Jun Du,Junyu Shi,Min Li,Mingqi Wu,Qiang Gao,Shangpeng Yan,Shaotong Qi,Shu Xu,Shuo Zhou,Tiankuo Xu,Tong Zheng,Weilun Zhao,Xiancheng Meng,Xianda Sun,Xiaoyu Jiang,Xunhao Jia,Yao Xia,Yimeng Xu,Yinghan Cui,Yingpeng Chen,Yiwen Ning,Yong Wang,Yuxuan Sun,Zhongsheng Liu,Ming Sun,Cheng Luo,Chen Yang,Han Li,Kun Gai*

Main category: cs.SE

TL;DR: KAT-Coder-V2.5 is an advanced coding agent model designed for autonomous operation within executable repositories. It addresses challenges in reproducibility and reward verification through a robust post-training framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation for developing KAT-Coder-V2.5 lies in enhancing the performance and autonomy of coding agents by addressing limitations in reproducible environments and high-value trajectories.

Method: The method employs an end-to-end agentic post-training framework, utilizing components like AutoBuilder for sandboxed verifications and KwaiClawEnv for synthesizing tool-use trajectories alongside reinforcement learning techniques.

Result: KAT-Coder-V2.5 achieved the best results in tool-use on the PinchBench and ranks second on repository-level software engineering, only behind Opus 4.8.

Conclusion: The findings confirm the effectiveness of KAT-Coder-V2.5 in achieving high levels of agentic tool-use and software engineering, marking significant advancements in coding-focused AI models.

Abstract: We present KAT-Coder-V2.5, a coding-focused agentic model trained to act autonomously inside real, executable repositories rather than as a single-turn code generator. Its capability is bottlenecked less by model scale than by the scarcity of reproducible environments, verifiable rewards, and high-value trajectories, which we address with an end-to-end agentic post-training framework. AutoBuilder reconstructs multilingual repositories into sandboxed environments with fail-to-pass and pass-to-pass verification at scale, from which we regenerate self-contained task specifications, recover near-miss trajectories, and distill supervision through process-aware filtering, while KwaiClawEnv synthesizes large-scale tool-use trajectories from executable services and real task seeds. We further scale reinforcement learning with harness randomization, a reliability-hardened sandbox, an asymmetric actor--critic PPO with hindsight-augmented value estimation, and a harness-oriented reward framework, and unify SWE, Agent-Claw, and WebCoding experts via Multi-Teacher On-Policy Distillation. Across six software-engineering and agentic benchmarks, KAT-Coder-V2.5 delivers the best agentic tool-use result on PinchBench and ranks second only to the frontier Opus 4.8 on repository-level software engineering. Our service is available at https://streamlake.com/product/kat-coder.

</details>


### [206] [TypeGo: An OS Runtime for Embodied Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05482)
*Guojun Chen,Alex Schott,Lin Zhong*

Main category: cs.SE

TL;DR: This paper proposes TypeGo, a runtime system for embodied agents leveraging LLMs to manage concurrent goals and real-time control through asynchronous planning and execution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The need to improve planning efficiency and responsiveness in embodied agents using LLMs while addressing the limitations of traditional request/response models.

Method: TypeGo employs asynchronous loops for planning at multiple timescales, managing agent behavior akin to an operating system, with features such as a Skill Kernel for resource arbitration, a scheduler for task management, and speculative skill streaming to reduce latency.

Result: The Kalos prototype, implemented on a Unitree Go2 quadruped, demonstrates a 50% reduction in per-step delay and a 73% decrease in time-to-first-action compared to traditional planning methods, allowing for concurrent task processing.

Conclusion: TypeGo significantly enhances the planning and execution efficiency of embodied agents, facilitating real-time interaction and improved responsiveness.

Abstract: Large language models (LLMs) can plan behavior for embodied agents from natural language, but treating the LLM as a request/response oracle on the critical path is fundamentally at odds with real-time control and concurrent goals. We argue for an operating-system-style runtime for embodied agents, and instantiate this idea in an early prototype, TypeGo.
